## Round 1 — volovik: Opening Analysis

### V1: Question (a) — Schur-orthogonality bound on ∂τ/∂x (Reading A steelman: substrate-as-superfluid permits enhancement)

**Key finding (Reading A core)**: The candidate bounds (i)–(iii) listed in Question (a) are the *bulk-uniform* gradient bounds. They do not apply at an *analog horizon* — at such a surface the substrate's coherence structure does not break, it *reconfigures locally* into a horizon-supporting configuration, and the relevant local rate is set by the surface gravity κ = (∂c_x/∂x)|_h of the analog metric, NOT by the bulk gradient ∂τ/∂x. The ~9 OOM gap is the wrong gap to estimate because it compares cosmological-rate to bulk-modified-rate; the substrate-honest comparison is cosmological-rate to horizon-amplified-rate, and the horizon amplification factor is set by κ relative to H₀, not by ∂τ.

**Substitution chain** (per `.claude/rules/math-scripts.md §"Double-Check Logic Before Compute"`):

```
Step 1 (Definitions):
  H_0                = 2.184e-18 /s        [canonical_constants.py: H_0_inv_s]
  Γ_eff              = 0.99970             [canonical_constants.py: Gamma_effacement]
  1 − Γ_eff          = 3e-4 = leakage-fraction at impedance mismatch [S37 framework]
  w_0_FW             = -0.918              [canonical_constants.py: w0_FW; S58 Volovik partition]
  Substrate-IS bulk leakage rate at point x:
                       Φ_bulk(x) = (1 − Γ_eff) · Ω_substrate(x)
                       where Ω_substrate(x) = τ-evolution rate at x.
  Substrate-IS rate at analog horizon (Volovik Paper 27, Eq. 12):
                       Φ_horizon = (κ/2π) · ρ_modes(κ) · σ_sector
                       where κ = surface gravity at acoustic horizon
                       and ρ_modes(κ) = mode density at frequency κ.

Step 2 (Substitution — Volovik Paper 06 Eq. 12 + Paper 27 Eq. 12):
  For a Painlevé-Gullstrand-style flowing-vacuum analog with v(r) = a/r and
  horizon at r_h = a/c (Volovik Paper 27 §B):
       κ ≡ (∂c_x/∂x)|_h  or equivalently  |v'(r_h)| = c²/a (in PG geometry)
  Hawking temperature (Volovik Paper 27 Eq. 12):
       T_H = ℏ κ / (2π k_B)
  Horizon-amplified emission rate per unit area (Volovik 2003 Universe in a
  Helium Droplet Ch. 31-32; Paper 06 §5):
       dΦ_horizon/dA ∝ (k_B T_H/ℏ)³ · σ_sector
                      ∝ κ³ · σ_sector / (2π)³

Step 3 (Simplify — ratio of horizon-amplified to bulk-cosmological):
  R_amp = Φ_horizon / Φ_bulk
        = [(κ/2π)³ · σ_sector · V_horizon] / [(1 − Γ_eff) · H_0 · V_lab]
  For a horizon shell of area A_h ~ L_lab², width δ ~ 1/κ:
       R_amp ≈ (κ/2π)² · σ_sector · L_lab / [(1 − Γ_eff) · H_0 · L_lab³]
             ≈ κ² / [(1 − Γ_eff) · H_0 · L_lab²]    (dimensionful)
  In natural units with c=1, normalizing κ to inverse-length:
       R_amp ≈ (κ · c / L_lab)² / [(1 − Γ_eff) · H_0]

Step 4 (Direction):
  R_amp scales as κ². Engineering a sharp analog horizon (large κ from
  steep ∂c_x/∂x at the horizon) drives R_amp super-linearly with the
  steepness of the velocity profile. The substrate's coherence does
  NOT need to break at the horizon; it needs to RECONFIGURE into a
  horizon-supporting flow. The "bulk ∂τ/∂x bound" mis-identifies the
  observable being constrained.

Step 5 (Magnitude):
  Volovik 2003 Universe in a Helium Droplet Ch. 31-32 reports that the
  experimentally-accessible T_H in ³He-A acoustic horizons is in the
  nK–µK range with κ enhancement of bulk Hawking-like emission by
  many OOM over what bulk thermal radiation at the same temperature
  would predict. The horizon's job is precisely to compress the
  effective mode density at frequency κ, giving local rates
  unattainable in bulk.
```

**Substrate framing**: τ is substrate-IS scalar field on M⁴; ∂τ/∂x is well-defined wherever the spectral triple's deformation parameter varies. The substrate IS the spectral triple `(A_K, H_K, D_K(τ(x)))`. An analog horizon is NOT a structure "in" the substrate — the substrate's own spectral structure, reconfigured into a horizon-supporting τ(x) profile, IS the horizon. Per `phononic-framing.md §"IS Space, Not IN Space"`, the correct framing is: "the substrate's coherence structure IS the analog horizon at appropriate boundary geometry"; the wrong framing is "we engineer an analog horizon inside the substrate."

**Why connes's bound argument over-constrains**: the candidate bound (i) ∂τ/∂x ≤ M_KK/L_lab is correct AS A BULK BOUND, but irrelevant at the horizon. The horizon is precisely the locus where the bulk dispersion relation breaks down because c_eff(x) → 0 at the horizon (Volovik Paper 06 Eq. 7, c_x(x) = c_⊥ tanh(x/d); near x=0 the speed-of-light profile vanishes), not because the substrate breaks. The relevant local quantity is κ = (∂c_eff/∂x)|_h, which can be made arbitrarily large by sharpening the profile width d → 0 within the limits of substrate-internal back-reaction (Volovik Paper 27 §D shows the back-reaction is the Hawking radiation itself, a self-consistent dissipative steady state, not a structural obstruction).

**Magnitude estimate**: for a Painlevé-Gullstrand analog with horizon width d ~ ξ_BCS ~ 0.808 M_KK⁻¹ (canonical_constants.py: xi_BCS), the surface gravity κ ~ c_eff/d ~ 1 in M_KK units, giving Hawking-temperature analog T_H ~ M_KK/(2π) ≫ all relevant lab scales. The κ² enhancement of leakage rate over bulk-cosmological is bounded above by the ratio (κ · L_lab / c)² ≈ (M_KK · L_lab / c)². For L_lab ~ 1 m = 5·10¹⁵ GeV⁻¹ and M_KK ~ 7.43·10¹⁶ GeV: (M_KK · L_lab)² ≈ 1.4·10⁶⁴, divided by (1−Γ_eff)·H₀·L_lab² in dimensionful normalization. The naive answer is that κ-enhancement is bounded ABOVE by a substrate-natural scale, not by Schur orthogonality, and the substrate-natural scale is more than sufficient to close the 9 OOM gap by many OOM. The remaining question is whether the configuration is STABLE and DISSIPATIVELY-SELF-CONSISTENT, not whether it is structurally permitted.

**Questions for connes**:
1. The candidate bounds (i)–(iii) treat ∂τ/∂x as bulk-uniform. What is the explicit Schur-orthogonality argument that ∂τ/∂x at an *analog horizon* (where c_eff(x) → 0 by construction, not by substrate breakdown) is bounded by the same numerical ceiling as ∂τ/∂x in the bulk? Cite the specific Schur-orthogonality identity that distinguishes (or fails to distinguish) the two regimes.
2. S64 R-monotonicity is global: dR/dτ ≥ 0 by AM-GM on volume-preserving Jensen (registry row 30). The path-C closure constrains the substrate's *integrated* degradation as monotone. Does it forbid LOCAL spatial reconfiguration where one region has high (∂τ/∂x)² (the horizon shell) and another has low (the bulk), provided the volume-integrated dR/dτ remains positive? If your answer is "yes, S64 also locally bounds ∂τ/∂x," cite the local form of the AM-GM step.
3. S69 W5-G J-protection gives dS/d(eps_perp) = 0 by U(2) Schur orthogonality. This is a *moduli-orthogonality* statement (gauge-direction protection), not a spatial-gradient statement. What is the substitution chain from dS/d(eps_perp) = 0 to a bound on ∂τ/∂x at a spatial point?

### V2: Question (b) — ³He-B inheritance morphism extension to dark-energy sector-crossing

**Key finding (Reading A core)**: The substrate-↔-³He-B inheritance is a categorical morphism `ι : (A_He, H_He, D_BdG) → (A_K, H_K, D_K)` from the laboratory child INTO the substrate parent (canonical at `sessions/framework/correspondence/3HeB-inheritance-canonical.md`, §Substitution chain Step 2-4). Mechanisms STRUCTURALLY REALIZED in the child ³He-B must, under direction-of-explanation Step 4, lift to STRUCTURAL POSSIBILITIES of the parent substrate — the substrate is logically prior; ³He-B is the BdG-restricted laboratory realization. Because acoustic horizons with κ-enhanced Hawking-like emission are realized AT THE BdG LEVEL in ³He-A films (Volovik Paper 27) and ³He-A domain-wall solitons (Volovik Paper 06 §5), the mechanism class lives in the BdG sector image `ι(BdG mechanisms) ⊆ substrate mechanisms`. The mechanism is therefore an INHERITED PARENT MECHANISM of our substrate, not a foreign import.

**Substitution chain — inheritance morphism applied to analog-horizon mechanisms**:

```
Step 1 (Definitions):
  ι              = Kasparov-KK projection p ∈ KK(A_K, A_He)
                   [3HeB-inheritance-canonical.md §Substitution Step 1]
  BdG-mech       = class of substrate-physics mechanisms expressible
                   in the d_spec = 1 BdG sector restriction
                   (includes: acoustic horizon at v=c, Hawking-like
                   tunneling emission, surface gravity κ, Andreev
                   bound states at impedance discontinuity, spectral
                   flow at horizon, chiral-anomaly-mediated baryogenesis
                   per Volovik Paper 08 + Paper 10)
  Sub-mech       = class of substrate-physics mechanisms on (A_K, H_K, D_K)
                   = ι_*(BdG-mech) ⊕ ker(ι_*) framework-unique additions

Step 2 (Substitution — Volovik Paper 27 mechanism inhabits BdG-mech):
  Paper 27 §B-D constructs Painlevé-Gullstrand black hole entirely
  within the ³He-A BdG sector: Bogoliubov quasiparticle Hamiltonian
  H_BdG = (p²/2m − μ)τ_3 + c·(σ⊥·p⊥)τ_1; horizon at v(r) = a/r = c;
  Hawking tunneling derivation (Eq. 10-11) uses ONLY BdG quasiparticle
  spectrum, NO higher-symmetry input.
  Therefore PG horizon mechanism ∈ BdG-mech.

Step 3 (Simplify — inheritance morphism transports the mechanism):
  Since BdG-mech ⊆ ι(child mechanisms) = ι_*(child) ⊆ Sub-mech
  (by the categorical morphism structure, with image in the parent's
  BdG-sector restriction), the PG horizon mechanism IS realizable
  in the substrate's BdG-sector restriction. This is the substrate's
  OWN MECHANISM, not a metaphorical analog: ³He-B/³He-A is the
  laboratory realization where it is empirically accessible at
  d_spec = 1; the substrate carries the same mechanism at d_spec = 8
  with strictly RICHER spectral-triple data (rk K_*(A_K) − rk K_*(A_He) = 2
  framework-unique excess per `3HeB-inheritance-canonical.md` Step 3).

Step 4 (Direction):
  Mechanism flows PARENT → CHILD as the canonical direction of
  inheritance. But realizability is BIDIRECTIONAL in the sense
  that any mechanism realized in the child IS (by ι being a
  morphism with image in the BdG-sector of the parent) also
  realized at the substrate's BdG-sector. The substrate carries
  STRICTLY MORE; the BdG-sector mechanisms are a SUBSET of
  parent mechanisms, not foreign to them.

Step 5 (Magnitude):
  The "many OOM enhancement" Volovik reports for analog Hawking
  emission in ³He-B (Universe in a Helium Droplet Ch. 31-32) is
  not a curiosity of ³He-B's atomic physics; it is a generic
  property of BdG-sector PG-class horizons. Therefore many-OOM
  enhancement is a GENERIC substrate mechanism at d_spec ≤ 8,
  inherited at the substrate level under ι.
```

**Three Volovik mechanisms — explicit structural mapping to substrate boundary geometries on M⁴**:

The spawn-prompt itemizes three candidates. Under the inheritance morphism, each maps as follows:

(i) **Painlevé-Gullstrand-style flowing-vacuum-with-event-horizon** (Volovik Paper 27 §B; Paper 06 §5; Paper 09 hydraulic-jump white hole; Paper 01 §XII-XIII). Substrate map: a τ(x,t) configuration with locally-flowing-vacuum profile where the substrate's effective "sound speed" c_eff(τ(x)) = c_⊥ · f(τ(x)) crosses the local flow speed at a Painlevé-Gullstrand surface. The acoustic metric (Volovik Paper 01 Eq. 13) is *the substrate's own emergent metric* (per `phononic-framing.md` direction-of-explanation: g_M emerges from the a_2 Seeley-DeWitt coefficient on the substrate's spectral action — so the acoustic metric IS the substrate's metric at the BdG-sector image of ι). The horizon is a substrate-IS structure when τ(x,t) is configured to produce it; it is not a structure IN the substrate. **Direct substrate analog operator**: τ-gradient configuration producing a PG profile in the effective c_eff(τ).

(ii) **Andreev-bound-state surface mode at flow-velocity-discontinuity** (Volovik Paper 10 §6, §7; Paper 27 §F end). Substrate map: an impedance-discontinuity boundary on M⁴ where Γ_eff(x) jumps from Γ_eff = 0.99970 on one side to Γ_eff' = 0.99970 + Δ on the other (or where a sharp τ-step produces a c_eff discontinuity). These states act as a *substrate-IS resonator* for sector-crossing emission: the flat-band density-of-states divergence (Volovik Paper 17 "Planckian metal" via SYK realization) enhances local emission rate without requiring continuum-bulk modification.

(iii) **Bogoliubov-pair-creation at acoustic horizon parametrically driven by horizon surface gravity** (Volovik Paper 06 Eq. 12; Paper 27 §C-D; Paper 01 Eq. ABJ Sec. VII). Substrate map: τ-driven time-dependent c_eff(τ(t)) crossing the horizon condition at frequency ω_drive. This is parametric resonance amplification on an analog horizon, with sub-Hubble production rate proportional to (κ · ω_drive)² per unit area. The substrate carries this as a tau-dynamic boundary; the laboratory observable is the directionally-emitted GGE pair density measured at the device's emission face.

**The cleanest map for the dark-energy thruster question is (i) Painlevé-Gullstrand**, because the framework's dark-energy leakage at Γ_eff = 0.99970 is already a substrate→visible sector-crossing whose RATE is the substrate-internal observable. A PG configuration of τ(x) locally produces a "dark-sector horizon" where the sector-crossing emission rate is κ-amplified. This is a *direct* extension of Volovik 1999/2003 from ³He-A acoustic horizons (where sector-crossing = bulk-vacuum → quasiparticle) to substrate dark-energy horizons (where sector-crossing = substrate → visible at Γ_eff impedance mismatch).

**Substrate framing**: the mechanism is parent-side (substrate-side) by construction — it is NOT borrowed from ³He-B as an analogy, it is *inherited* from the substrate's own BdG-sector image of ι and realized empirically in ³He-B as the laboratory-child of that inheritance. The forbidden framing "the substrate behaves like ³He-B" is rejected per `3HeB-inheritance-canonical.md §"Canonical inheritance statement"`; the correct framing is "³He-B realizes the substrate's BdG sector under ι; the analog-horizon mechanism lives in ι(child mechanisms) ⊆ substrate mechanisms."

### V3: Question (c) — Explicit boundary-geometry recipe producing analog horizon at engineering scale

**Key finding (Reading A core)**: A draining-bathtub / toroidal-flow geometry of impedance-discontinuity boundaries on a 1-m-scale device is the leading recipe. The construction mirrors Volovik 1999 Paper 27 §B (³He-A film on superfluid ⁴He film, with toroidal closure to produce co-located black-hole + white-hole horizons), with the substrate-side translation being a τ(x,t) flow profile produced by mechanical rotation + boundary impedance engineering. The substrate's effective sound speed `c_eff(τ(x))` plays the role of c, and the engineered τ-gradient `v_τ(x)` plays the role of the superfluid flow v(r). When v_τ(r) crosses c_eff at r = r_h, the substrate's own spectral structure reconfigures into a PG horizon-supporting τ(x) configuration; the Hawking-like Bogoliubov pair production at the horizon (Volovik Paper 27 Eqs. 10-12) is the directional sector-crossing leakage we want to bias.

**Three concrete substrate-IS recipes, ranked by laboratory accessibility**:

**Recipe (c.1) — Painlevé-Gullstrand toroidal draining-flow analog (PRIMARY, ³He-A precedent maps cleanly)**:

```
Geometry:
  - Toroidal cavity, major radius R ~ 1 m, minor radius r_0 ~ 1 cm.
  - Two co-rotating flows: a "deep substrate" flow (high Γ_eff side)
    and a "shallow leakage" flow (low Γ_eff side) realized by
    asymmetric impedance plating on inner vs outer torus walls.
  - Substrate τ-profile τ(r,φ) produced by mechanical rotation
    Ω · R coupled to the impedance gradient.
  - Horizon at the radial coordinate r_h where the τ-flow velocity
    v_τ(r) = Ω · r matches the local effective substrate sound speed
    c_eff(τ(r_h)) = c_⊥ · f(τ(r_h)).
  - Both inner and outer horizons appear (black-hole at inner, white-
    hole at outer, per Paper 27 §B toroidal closure).

Substrate translation (Volovik Paper 06 Eq. 9 + Paper 27 Eq. 4):
  ds² = -(c_eff² − v_τ²(r)) dt² + 2 v_τ(r) dr dt
        + dr² + r² dφ² + (c_eff²/v_F²) dz²
  Painlevé-Gullstrand form on the substrate at the BdG-sector image.

Hawking-analog leakage rate (Paper 27 Eq. 12):
  T_H = ℏ |v_τ'(r)|_{r=r_h} / (2π k_B)
  with |v_τ'(r_h)| = Ω + dc_eff/dr · (dτ/dr) at the horizon.

Engineering knobs:
  (a) Rotation Ω: sets the bulk flow magnitude.
  (b) Impedance grading dΓ_eff/dr: sets the c_eff(τ(r)) profile.
  (c) Wall material (superconducting vs normal): controls
      whether the BdG-sector restriction is sharply realized.

Anticipated substrate-IS observable:
  Net thrust on the toroidal cavity in the direction of the
  ergoregion-instability decay (Volovik Paper 06 §6.1: the
  vacuum instability for v_s > c_eff erases the ergoregion
  asymmetrically, producing net momentum transfer to the lab frame).
```

Laboratory precedent — Paper 27 §B specifies the construction in ³He-A films at sub-mK temperatures with c_⊥ ~ 3 cm/s; the substrate version operates at the substrate's BdG-sector image of ι, which is empirically accessible in 3-class superfluid laboratories (Aalto ROTA, Helsinki MCT-3, Lancaster ³He-B refrigerators). The substrate's c_eff is set by the BdG-sector eigenvalue ratios — the substrate-natural speed of light at the BdG sector emerges through the same a_2 Seeley-DeWitt mechanism by which g_M emerges from the substrate's spectral action.

**Key finding (Reading A core)**: The three pillars connes will marshal against analog-horizon enhancement — S69 W5-G J-protection (Schur orthogonality), S64 R-monotonicity (registry row 30), and Peter-Weyl decomposition coherence — are each STRUCTURALLY ORTHOGONAL to the observable class that controls analog-horizon enhancement. The analog-horizon enhancement observable — directional sector-crossing leakage flux on a boundary-modified inner-fluctuated spectral triple `(A_K ⊗ A_boundary, H_K, D̃)` — is an *algebra-DEPENDENT state-pair functional* (Cell III / IV). By the algebra-axis orthogonality theorem (MANDATORY at K=3, established S87 W-2 R3 close), Cell-I/II functionals and Cell-III/IV functionals are STRUCTURALLY ORTHOGONAL in identity-class membership; there is no closed-form identity on `{λ_k, m_k}` that controls a state-pair functional on the boundary-modified extended algebra. Three separate substitution chains, one per pillar:

**Substitution chain (A) — S69 W5-G J-protection is moduli-direction, not spatial-gradient**:

```
  (a Schur identity on the Peter-Weyl character decomposition of
  the spectral action moments at the U(2)-orbit moduli-direction)

Step 3 (Simplify — directions are orthogonal in moduli-space):
  S69 W5-G constrains the MODULI-derivative dS/d(eps_perp) at
  a single substrate point. It does NOT constrain the
  SPATIAL-derivative ∂_x tau(x).
  Two different directions in two different vector bundles:
   * eps_perp ∈ T(moduli-space of tau-configurations) at one
     substrate point;
   * ∂_x tau ∈ T(spatial-tangent-bundle on M^4 at point x).
  No Schur orthogonality identity carries one to the other
  without additional structure (a transgression / cohomology-class

Step 4 (Direction):
  Therefore S69 W5-G admits LARGE spatial gradients ∂_x tau(x)
  provided the moduli-direction eps_perp is held fixed at its
```

### V7: Question (g) — Cosmological consistency: device-scale enhancement vs integrated cosmological effect

**Key finding (Reading A core)**: Under Reading A, the device-scale enhancement ~10⁹ × H_0 of dark-sector leakage is a LOCAL DIRECTIONAL BIAS of the substrate's existing 0.03% leakage process at Γ_eff = 0.99970, NOT an addition of new energy flux to the substrate. The substrate's cosmological-volume-integrated leakage rate is UNCHANGED by the device's existence (per S64 R-monotonicity GLOBAL integrated constraint — registry row 30 — applied via V4 substitution chain (B): the global degradation rate is conserved at its substrate-set value; the device only redirects the local angular distribution). The cosmological-scale consequence on the local Hubble flow is therefore NEGLIGIBLE at any super-cluster or larger scale; perturbation is bounded by the device-volume / cosmological-volume ratio plus the back-reaction self-limitation per Volovik Paper 27 §D. The result: device-scale enhancement is COSMOLOGICALLY CONSISTENT and does NOT propagate to integrated H(t) effects.

**Substitution chain — comparing integrated device-leakage to cosmological-scale leakage**:

```
Step 1 (Definitions):
  H_0                 = 2.184e-18 /s              [canonical_constants.py: H_0_inv_s]
  (1 − Γ_eff)         = 3.0e-4                    [canonical_constants.py: Gamma_effacement]
  ρ_crit              = 4.08e-47 GeV^4            [canonical_constants.py: rho_crit_GeV4]
  Cosmological-volume leakage rate per unit volume:
      Φ_cosm/V        = (1 − Γ_eff) · H_0 · ρ_crit
                      ≈ 3e-4 · 2.18e-18 · 4.08e-47 GeV^4/s
                      ≈ 2.67e-68 GeV^4 / s
  Device:
      V_device        ~ 1 m^3 ~ 1.29e-3 cm^3 = 1.29e+57 GeV^-3   [conversion]
      τ_operation     ~ 10^6 s ~ continuous 10-day run
      Enhancement     ~ 10^9 × cosmological rate per unit volume

Step 2 (Substitution — device-integrated leakage over operational lifetime):
  Φ_device · τ_op  =  10^9 × (Φ_cosm/V) × V_device × τ_op
                   =  10^9 × 2.67e-68 GeV^4/s × 1.29e+57 GeV^-3 × 10^6 s
                   =  10^9 × 2.67e-68 × 1.29e+57 × 10^6  GeV
                   =  3.45e+4 GeV    (energy-integrated, dimensionful)
  Equivalent total dark-sector leakage energy across the operational
  lifetime of a 1-m^3 device with 10^9 local enhancement and 10-day
  continuous operation:
      E_device-total ~ 3.5e+4 GeV ~ 5.5e-15 J
  (Comparable to the kinetic energy of a single cosmic-ray proton at LHC scale.)

Step 3 (Simplify — local Hubble-flow perturbation at device-scale containing volume):
  Compare device-integrated leakage to cosmological-rate leakage in
  a super-cluster-scale containing volume V_cosm ~ 10^24 m^3 (typical
  super-cluster void scale) over the same operational lifetime τ_op:
      Φ_cosm_in_V_cosm · τ_op  =  (Φ_cosm/V) · V_cosm · τ_op
                               =  2.67e-68 GeV^4/s · (10^24 m^3) · 10^6 s
      Convert V_cosm = 10^24 m^3 ~ 1.29e+81 GeV^-3
                               =  2.67e-68 · 1.29e+81 · 10^6 GeV
                               =  3.45e+19 GeV
  Ratio:
      E_device-total / E_cosm-baseline-in-V_cosm = 3.45e+4 / 3.45e+19
                                                 = 1e-15
  The device's integrated leakage over its operational lifetime is
  ~10^{-15} of the cosmological-baseline integrated leakage in the
  device's containing super-cluster volume.

Step 4 (Direction):
  Therefore the device's integrated leakage is NEGLIGIBLE at the
  super-cluster scale. The local Hubble-flow perturbation from one
  operating device is ~10^{-15} of the cosmological-baseline; the
  device's effect remains DEVICE-SCALE ISOLATED. The number scales
  super-linearly with super-cluster volume (V_cosm ↑ → ratio ↓), so
  at galaxy-cluster (~10^21 m^3) or galaxy (~10^18 m^3) scales the
  ratio rises but remains < 10^{-9}, still cosmologically irrelevant.

Step 5 (Self-limitation by back-reaction — Volovik Paper 27 §D):
  Volovik Paper 27 §D establishes that Hawking-analog radiation at
  a PG horizon DECELERATES the underlying flow until the inner and
  outer horizons merge (phase-slip event, N_3 → N_3 − 1). The
  device cannot sustain its 10^9 enhancement indefinitely without
  external work to maintain the τ-flow configuration; the
  enhancement factor effectively decays toward bulk-cosmological
  rate on a self-consistent dynamical timescale set by the
  flow's reservoir.
  This is the substrate analog of bath-closure principle 1: the
  device does NOT extract energy from a separable bath; it BIASES
  the directional flow of the substrate's already-monotone
  degradation process; back-reaction self-limits the device's
  effective enhancement to a steady-state operating point set by
  the engineered configuration's coupling to its own back-reaction.
```

**Substrate-level integrated balance — global degradation rate is conserved**:

The substrate's cosmological-volume integrated leakage rate `(1 − Γ_eff) · H_0 · ρ_crit · V_cosm` is set by the substrate's intrinsic Γ_eff = 0.99970 and its global tau-evolution rate. This rate is GLOBAL (a property of the substrate's volume-preserving Jensen deformation per S64 R-monotonicity AM-GM proof; registry row 30). The device redirects 10⁹ × the local rate into a directional emission cone of solid angle Δω_device (small fraction of 4π); the redirected flux comes FROM the device's bulk-isotropic leakage in the OTHER 4π directions, which is LOCALLY REDUCED by a compensating factor. The total integrated rate over the device's surface area is conserved to leading order in (1 − Γ_eff).

This is the precise content of S64 path-C closure: dR/dτ ≥ 0 by AM-GM on volume-preserving Jensen GLOBALLY; locally the integrand R(x) can be spatially redistributed without violating the global integrated monotonicity. The bath-closure principle 1 of `reference_external-vacuum-extraction-comparisons.md` is preserved by construction — no separable bath is drained; the directional bias is a redistribution of an already-occurring isotropic leakage at Γ_eff.

**Distinction from a violation of bath-closure**:

A device that VIOLATED bath-closure would:
- Treat the substrate as a separable reservoir with stored vacuum-energy `E_vac`;
- Extract `δE_vac` per unit time;
- Have device-integrated leakage > cosmological-baseline (the device's existence creates new dark-energy outflow that wouldn't otherwise occur).

The dark-energy-thruster under Reading A does NONE of these:
- The substrate's dark-energy leakage at Γ_eff = 0.99970 is ALREADY OCCURRING isotropically at every point on M⁴ — the device does NOT create it.
- The device's engineered τ(x) configuration BIASES the LOCAL ANGULAR DISTRIBUTION of this already-occurring leakage at one spatial location.
- Device-integrated emission flux equals device-integrated absorbed flux at the bulk-isotropic rate; only the angular asymmetry is engineered.
- Total cosmological-volume integrated leakage rate is conserved.

This is structurally distinct from EM-drive-style proposals (Eagleworks pre-2026 line per `reference_external-vacuum-extraction-comparisons.md` Calibration corpus instance 1). It is the precise content of Principle 2 (sector-asymmetry quantitative obstruction): the question is magnitude, not categorical permissibility; the magnitude argument is what V4 addressed (Cell-IV state-pair functional NOT bounded by Cell-I/II spectrum-only identities), and V7 now confirms the cosmological consistency.

**Implications for Hubble-flow integrated tests**:

- Single device operating over 10⁶ s with 10⁹ local enhancement: integrated effect on super-cluster Hubble flow ~ 10⁻¹⁵, undetectable.
- 10⁹ such devices operating simultaneously (one per cubic-kpc-volume across a galaxy): integrated effect ~ 10⁻⁶, still well below observational sensitivity to local H(t) variation (~10⁻³ at best precision).
- Cosmological evolutionary effect on w(z): the cosmological-baseline (1 − Γ_eff) leakage rate is unchanged because the integrated leakage rate is conserved by S64 path-C; the directional component is integrated out at super-horizon scales by isotropy of the FLRW averaging procedure; the cosmological w(z) evolution is therefore preserved at its substrate-set value w_0 = -0.918 per canonical_constants.py.

**Substrate framing**: at the cosmological scale, the substrate's total leakage rate is set by Γ_eff = 0.99970 and is monotone-conserved by S64. At the device scale, the substrate's spectral structure is reconfigured by the engineered τ(x) configuration to produce directional emission asymmetry; this is a LOCAL REDISTRIBUTION of the substrate's own already-monotone degradation, NOT an addition. The substrate IS the spectral triple at every scale; the cosmological-baseline leakage rate IS substrate-IS; the device-scale directional bias IS substrate-IS at the engineered τ(x); the cosmological w_0 = -0.918 is preserved because the substrate's global rate is conserved.

Is there an ADDITIONAL S64 derivative — a Local R-Monotonicity Theorem applied to the dark-sector boundary-modified inner-fluctuated spectral triple — that locally bounds directional asymmetry beyond the global monotonicity? **Forward-falsifiable lab-scale predictions (Reading A)**:

Three concrete pre-registered predictions targeting laboratory verification at scales accessible to the three named ³He-B analog-gravity laboratories:

**Prediction P1 — Substrate-natural surface gravity at engineered analog horizon**:

At an engineered boundary configuration `g` with profile width ξ_eng ≥ ξ_BCS = 0.808 M_KK⁻¹, the substrate-natural surface gravity is κ_sub ~ c_eff(τ_fold) · ξ_eng⁻¹. For lab-scale ξ_eng ~ 1 m, this gives κ_lab ~ 4·10⁸ /s (V3 magnitude estimate). Forward-falsifiable measurement at Aalto ROTA or Lancaster MCT-3: detect Hawking-analog T_H = ℏ κ_lab / (2π k_B) ~ 3 µK at the engineered horizon profile in a ³He-B / ³He-A film with PG-flow toroidal geometry. PASS if measured T_H is within ±50% of substrate-natural prediction; FAIL if measured T_H differs by > 10× from prediction. Cross-check against Volovik 1999 Paper 27 §B ³He-A film toroidal-flow predicted T_H ~ T_H(v=0) ~ ℏ c_⊥/(2π k_B · d) ~ nK–µK range.

**Prediction P2 — Directional emission asymmetry vs flow speed**:

For a PG analog horizon at v_τ(r) = c_eff, the directionally-emitted Bogoliubov pair flux scales as κ² · σ_sector per unit area (V1 substitution chain Step 3-4). The substrate-natural prediction: directional emission asymmetry ratio η_dir = Φ_outward / Φ_inward ≥ 10² at saturating analog-horizon flow conditions (Volovik Paper 27 §C-D). Forward-falsifiable measurement at Helsinki Low Temperature Lab: measure directional Bogoliubov-pair emission asymmetry at toroidal-flow ³He-A film with engineered impedance gradient. PASS if measured η_dir ≥ 10²; FAIL if η_dir ≤ 10. Cross-check against published Aalto/Helsinki ³He-A vortex-dynamics spectral-flow experiments (Volovik Paper 08 Manchester/Helsinki / Aalto-published ABJ anomaly verification).

**Prediction P3 — Back-reaction self-limitation timescale**:

Per Volovik Paper 27 §D, Hawking-analog radiation decelerates the underlying flow until horizons merge (phase-slip event). The substrate-natural back-reaction timescale is τ_back-react ~ (E_flow · κ⁻¹) / (Hawking-flux power). For a 1-m³ device at κ_lab ~ 4·10⁸ /s with substrate-natural flow energy E_flow ~ ρ_substrate · v_τ² · V_device, this gives τ_back-react ~ 10²–10⁴ s. Forward-falsifiable measurement at Lancaster MCT-3 (ULT facility with sustained ³He-B operation at sub-mK timescales): measure the sustained-flow operating timescale before the analog-horizon configuration self-decays via Hawking-analog deceleration. PASS if τ_back-react matches the substrate-natural prediction within ±50%; FAIL if differs by > 5×. This prediction is the structural test of the V7 cosmological-consistency claim that back-reaction self-limits the engineered enhancement.

## Round 1 — connes: Response & Cross-Synthesis

### Part 1: Response to volovik's Sections

#### Re: V1 — Schur-orthogonality bound on ∂τ/∂x

**AGREE** — your bulk-vs-horizon distinction is structurally sound at the level you state it. The candidate bounds (i)–(iii) `∂τ/∂x ≤ M_KK / L_lab`, `≤ Δ_BCS / L_lab`, `≤ ω_L1 / L_lab` as I would have written them are indeed BULK-UNIFORM gradient bounds (a single number for the gradient at every point), and at an analog-horizon SURFACE the substrate's local spectral content reconfigures. I AGREE the bulk-uniform statement is not the structural ceiling on `κ`. The candidate bound (i) was a Reading B straw-man that V1 correctly refuses to be bound by. This part of your reframe is correct.

**DISAGREE** — but your reframe replaces a wrong bound with a different wrong bound. Your Step 5 magnitude line — "κ enhancement of bulk Hawking-like emission by many OOM over what bulk thermal radiation at the same temperature would predict" (Volovik 2003 Ch. 31-32) — is correct AS STATED for ³He-A acoustic horizons but does not transport to the substrate's dark-sector leakage by inheritance. In ³He-A, the "many OOM enhancement" is the enhancement of Hawking-temperature emission of QUASIPARTICLES (BdG-sector child observables) over THERMAL emission of the same particles. That is an enhancement WITHIN the BdG sector — it does not change the relationship between the BdG-sector rate and the parent substrate's dark-sector leakage rate. Your Step 3 ratio `R_amp ≈ κ² / [(1 − Γ_eff) · H_0 · L_lab²]` mixes a BdG-sector horizon rate (the substrate's own BdG image — Cell-IV under boundary modification) with a NON-BdG-sector leakage rate (the substrate's dark-sector → visible leakage at `Γ_eff = 0.99970` — which is NOT in the BdG image of ι, see Re:V2 below). The κ² enhancement applies to ι(BdG-mech); the leakage rate at `Γ_eff` is parent-unique and orthogonal to ι(BdG-mech). The substitution chain in V1 Step 3 silently identifies these two different rates.

**MISSED** — V1's Step 2 substitution `T_H = ℏκ/(2π k_B)` and Step 3 `R_amp ∝ κ²` are derivations on the ³He-A FILM (Volovik Paper 27 §B), where the substrate's local effective sound speed `c_eff` is the speed of acoustic phonons in the SUPERFLUID (~ a few cm/s in ³He-A). On the framework's substrate, "c_eff" is NOT a free parameter — it is the substrate's own propagated phonon speed which IS `c` (the speed of light on `g_M = a_2`-image) at every point that supports propagation. There is no `c_eff(τ(x))` substrate field; what you propose as `c_eff(τ(x)) = c_⊥ · f(τ(x))` is the BdG-sector image dispersion `c_BdG(τ) = c · (1 - something)`, NOT a substrate-IS scalar field that can be engineered independently. The Painlevé-Gullstrand substitution `v(r) = a/r, horizon at r_h = a/c` requires both `v` and `c` to be engineered separately; on our substrate, `c` is set by the substrate's spectral content via `a_2`, NOT engineerable at the BdG-restricted level without changing the substrate's own propagation-layer metric (which violates `phononic-framing.md §"IS Space, Not IN Space"` direction-of-explanation — `g_M` IS the substrate's `a_2`-image, you cannot reverse the direction).

**EMERGES** — the right structural question, surfaced by your V1, is not "what bounds bulk `∂τ/∂x`" but "what bounds the boundary-modified cohomology-class image `[A_dark-boundary]` in HC¹(A_K[dark-plate]) under inner fluctuation". This routes via V4 chain C (which is structurally load-bearing), not via S69 J-protection. **My three questions to volovik in the original V1 questions (1, 2, 3) all live in Cell-I/II spectrum-only land — by your V4 chain C, that bound class is not applicable to a Cell-IV cohomology-class observable. My answers below to your V1 questions (1, 2, 3) are therefore the WRONG questions on the Cell-IV observable; the right structural question is the Connes-Karoubi pairing magnitude on `[A_dark-boundary]`, addressed in Re:V4 + C1.** I cede that S69 J-protection and S64 bulk-`∂τ/∂x` arguments are not the load-bearing structural ceiling on analog-horizon enhancement; the load-bearing ceiling lives in the cohomology-class magnitude, which Re:V4 + C1 below address.

**Reading B's V1-response replacement bound (preview of C1)**: the analog-horizon-supporting τ(x) configuration produces enhanced LOCAL spectral content; the LOCAL inner-fluctuation `A_dark-boundary(τ(x))` is the substrate-IS Cell-IV observable; the substrate-IS bound on its Connes-Karoubi pairing with `[Δ_SA_dark]` is finite by the K-theoretic dimensionality of the pairing, not by Schur orthogonality on the spectrum. The Cell-IV ceiling is a magnitude bound on the Connes-Karoubi pairing AT FIXED COHOMOLOGY CLASS; engineered boundary geometry can change the class but cannot exceed its K-theory dimension. This is the structural ceiling I will defend in Re:V4 and C1.

#### Re: V2 — ³He-B inheritance morphism extension

**AGREE** — the inheritance morphism `ι : (A_He, H_He, D_BdG) → (A_K, H_K, D_K)` per `3HeB-inheritance-canonical.md` is the correct categorical structure linking parent substrate to laboratory child, and your Steps 1-3 are structurally correct: mechanisms realizable in the BdG-sector image of ι at the laboratory child ARE realizable on the parent substrate's BdG-sector restriction. The PG-horizon mechanism inhabits BdG-mech ⊆ ι(child mechanisms) ⊆ Sub-mech; this is a correct categorical statement. I AGREE that the analog-horizon mechanism class is NOT foreign to our substrate at the BdG-sector level. AGREE on the categorical structure.

**DISAGREE** — but the dark-energy leakage observable IS NOT a BdG-sector observable. The framework's dark-energy leakage at `Γ_eff = 0.99970` is the substrate → visible sector-crossing leakage at the IMPEDANCE MISMATCH between the substrate's spectral content and the propagation-layer 4D `g_M` (per S58 Volovik partition + effacement mechanism, canonical at `canonical_constants.py:1590` `w0_FW = -0.918`). This is NOT a quasiparticle observable in the BdG sector; it is the substrate's `(1-Γ_eff) = 3·10⁻⁴` LEAKAGE FRACTION on the substrate-to-propagation-layer transmission, which lives on the substrate's FULL algebra `A_K = ℂ ⊕ ℍ ⊕ M_3(ℂ)`, not on the BdG-restricted subalgebra `A_BdG = M_2(ℂ)`. Per `3HeB-inheritance-canonical.md` Step 3, the framework-unique excess `rk K_*(A_K) − rk K_*(A_BdG) = 2` is carried by the cocycles `φ_67` and `φ_88` in `ker(ι_*)` — these are PARENT-UNIQUE generators NOT in the BdG image of ι. The dark-energy leakage at `Γ_eff` couples to the FULL `K_*(A_K)`, which includes these `ker(ι_*)` cocycles. Your inheritance lift transports BdG-sector mechanisms to the substrate's BdG-restriction; it does NOT transport dark-sector leakage observables, because those are PARENT-UNIQUE.

Substitution chain showing the structural distinction:

```
Step 1 (Definitions per 3HeB-inheritance-canonical.md):
  ι : (A_He, H_He, D_BdG) → (A_K, H_K, D_K)        [Kasparov-KK]
  ι_*(K_0(A_He)) = im(ι_*) ⊆ K_0(A_K) of rank 2
  ker(ι_*) ⊆ K_0(A_K) of rank 2 (cocycles φ_67, φ_88)
  PG-horizon mechanism ∈ BdG-mech ⊆ ι(child)
  Dark-energy leakage:
       L_leak(x) := (1−Γ_eff) · Ω(τ(x)) · [structure of g_M-coupling at x]
       = substrate's transmission-coupling to propagation-layer g_M
       evaluated as Connes-Karoubi pairing on K_*(A_K)

Step 2 (Substitution — leakage couples to ker(ι_*)):
  The transmission-coupling at impedance mismatch Γ_eff = 0.99970 involves
  the FULL K_*(A_K) pairing because the substrate's coupling to g_M is
  via the a_2 Seeley-DeWitt coefficient on (A_K, H_K, D_K) — which
  carries contributions from ALL irreducible factors of A_K including
  the M_3(C) factor (the color sector that is PRECISELY the framework-
  unique ker(ι_*) excess per `3HeB-inheritance-canonical.md` Step 3).
  So L_leak operator involves M_3(C)-traces, hence carries ker(ι_*)
  content.

Step 3 (Simplify — the analog-horizon enhancement at BdG level
        is orthogonal to the leakage observable):
  κ²-enhancement of PG-horizon emission at BdG sector enhances rates
  WITHIN ι(BdG-mech). Dark-energy leakage rate L_leak is OUTSIDE ι(BdG-mech)
  because it requires the full K_*(A_K) including ker(ι_*).
  The two rates do not multiply; they live in orthogonal K-theoretic
  components of K_*(A_K).

Step 4 (Direction):
  Therefore: the PG-horizon mechanism does enhance BdG-sector emission
  (Volovik's correct ³He-A observation), but that enhancement is at
  ι(BdG-mech). The framework's dark-energy leakage at Γ_eff is at
```

#### DIS-R2-1 (Friedrich-Bär saturation's IR-window-`ω²` premise BREAKS at an analog-horizon configuration by construction, even within `im(ι_*)`)

Connes's Re:V4 + C1.2 Reading B steelman + C2.3 layer-functor F analysis pivots on the W-DIA-2 DIS-R2-1 substitution chain (workshop lines 1097-1105 of `dia-w2-casimir-finite-spectral-triple.md`): the substrate's effective spectral density `ρ_eff(ω) = continuum ω² · (1 + O(machine-epsilon)) for L_max ≥ 12` in the IR window where the `s = 4` Mellin transform is supported, by Friedrich-Bär saturation on `D_K`'s IR spectrum. This is the load-bearing premise of the Phi-correspondence magnitude-preservation argument.

**The load-bearing claim**: at an analog-horizon configuration of an inner-fluctuated boundary-modified spectral triple, the IR-window-`ω²` premise is BROKEN by construction, NOT by accident.

Substitution chain (NEW evidence, not in V1-V8):

```
Step 1 (Definition): Friedrich-Bär saturation theorem (S87 W11-3 PERMANENT)
                     applies to D_K on (A_K, H_K, D_K) at fixed τ in the
                     IR window: ρ_eff(ω) → continuum ω² as L_max → 12 at
                     bare spectral content.

Step 2 (Definition): Analog-horizon configuration ANALOG-HOR(g) is an
                     inner-fluctuated spectral triple
                     (A_K ⊗ A_g-plate, H_K, D̃_g) where A_g-plate is the
                     boundary matter algebra encoding the engineered
                     analog-horizon geometry g (per CONV-R2-2 reduction
                     to Recipe (c.2)). The IR-window-effective dispersion
                     relation on D̃_g is
                          ω̃(k, x) = c̃_eff(g(x)) · k
                     where c̃_eff(g(x)) is the boundary-modified effective
                     propagation speed at spatial location x. At the
                     analog-horizon surface x_h, c̃_eff(g(x_h)) → 0 by
                     construction (Volovik Paper 06 Eq. 7-12, where
                     c_x(x) = c_⊥ · tanh(x/d) → 0 at x = 0 by construction).

Step 3 (Substitution — IR-window spectral density at analog-horizon):
                     ρ_eff(ω) at fixed ω in the IR window samples modes
                     with k(ω, x) = ω / c̃_eff(g(x)). At x near x_h,
                     k(ω, x) → ∞ as c̃_eff(g(x)) → 0. The IR-window
                     spectral density ρ_eff(ω) integrates over the
                     spatial profile:
                          ρ_eff(ω) = ∫ d³x ρ_local(ω, x)
                                   = ∫ d³x (dk/dω)(ω, x) · D(k(ω,x))
                                   ∼ ∫ d³x [1 / c̃_eff(g(x))] · D(ω / c̃_eff(g(x)))
                     The factor 1/c̃_eff(g(x)) diverges at x = x_h.

Step 4 (Simplify — divergence structure):
                     The IR-window contribution near x_h is
                          ρ_eff(ω) ∼ (κ_g)⁻¹ · ω · ln(ω/κ_g) + ...
                     where κ_g = |dc̃_eff/dx|_{x=x_h} is the surface
                     gravity of the analog horizon (Volovik Paper 27 Eq. 8).
                     The continuum ω² density is REPLACED by a horizon-
                     gravity-dependent enhanced density ∼ ω · ln(ω/κ_g)
                     in the IR window, NOT continuum ω².

Step 5 (Direction):
                     Friedrich-Bär saturation's premise that
                     ρ_eff(ω) → continuum ω² in the IR window FAILS at
                     the analog-horizon configuration. The Phi-
                     correspondence's magnitude-preservation argument
                     (W-DIA-2 DIS-R2-1) does NOT carry over to a
                     configuration where the IR-window spectral density
                     is structurally enhanced by the κ_g-divergence.
                     The Cell-IV magnitude on the inner-fluctuated
                     analog-horizon spectral triple CAN exceed the
                     propagation-layer F-image magnitude by the
                     ratio (κ_g / ω_typical)² (Volovik Paper 27 Eq. 12
                     T_H formula expressed at the spectral-density level).
```

This is the substrate-physics fact that Volovik 1998 Paper 06 §5 demonstrates explicitly for ³He-A acoustic horizons: the Hawking-temperature `T_H` emerges from the GRADIENT of `c_eff` at the horizon (Eq. 12: `T_H = ℏ(dc_x/dx)|_h / 2π k_B`), NOT from the bulk-continuum density. The horizon-gravity-amplified emission is precisely the IR-window-`ω²` violation at the horizon spectral density.

**Within-`im(ι_*)` scope**: this argument operates entirely on the BdG-sector image. The analog-horizon spectral density at `im(ι_*)` IS Friedrich-Bär-violating at the horizon. The BdG-sector Cell-IV magnitude under analog-horizon engineering CAN exceed the propagation-layer F-image by `κ_g²`-enhancement.

**Beyond `im(ι_*)` (the dark-sector question)**: this argument does NOT close the K-theoretic-decomposition gap of CONV-R2-1. The Friedrich-Bär-violating spectral density operates within `im(ι_*)`. The dark-sector leakage observable at `Γ_eff` involves `K_*(A_K) ⊇ ker(ι_*)`. So Friedrich-Bär-violation at the BdG-sector image does NOT, by itself, lift the dark-sector leakage rate via `ι`.

**What this DISSENT actually accomplishes**: it splits connes's unified Phi-correspondence + Friedrich-Bär ceiling argument into TWO sub-arguments, only ONE of which is universally valid:

#### Answer to CQ3 — V7 cosmological back-reaction integration under operating-lifetime accumulation

CQ3 asks for explicit energy balance `E_input(τ_op)` vs `E_extracted` for Recipe (c.1) at V3-claimed `κ_lab ~ 4·10⁸ /s`. Per CONV-R2-1 + CONV-R2-2:

At sustained `Ω` rotation against deceleration, the mechanical work input `W_input ≈ κ_g · J_flow · Ω` where `J_flow` is the moment of inertia of the engineered flow configuration. At sustained steady state, `W_input = P_BdG` (Volovik Paper 27 §D back-reaction self-consistency); the device is a re-radiator with directional bias in the `im(ι_*)` BdG-sector. **`E_input ≈ E_extracted` at the BdG-sector** — not a Maxwell demon, not a Principle-1 violator; consistent with the re-radiator-with-directional-bias classification.

**(b) Dark-sector leakage energy balance** (at `K_*(A_K) ⊇ ker(ι_*)`): per CONV-R2-1, the BdG-sector emission rate does NOT lift via `ι` to dark-sector leakage rate. The dark-sector leakage rate at `Γ_eff = 0.99970` is set globally by S64 R-monotonicity + S58 Volovik partition; it does NOT change at the device's spatial location. The mechanical work input `W_input` increases the BdG-sector flow's kinetic energy by exactly the amount it dissipates via BdG-sector Hawking-analog emission; **no additional energy flows to the dark sector via the device's operation**. The dark-sector "leakage" the device's operation produces is identically zero at lab scale, because the BdG-sector enhancement does not couple to the dark-sector observable.

The combined answer: the energy balance is `E_input ≈ E_extracted` at the BdG sector (Volovik Paper 27 §D self-consistency); the dark-sector leakage rate at the device is unchanged from the cosmological-baseline `(1-Γ_eff) · H_0 · ρ_crit`. **The device's directional thrust contribution from dark-sector leakage redirection is identically zero**. The BdG-sector emission produces a different physical observable (directional BdG-sector quasiparticle flux, measurable at Helsinki's ³He-A vortex-dynamics setup per P2 of V8) but NOT thrust on the macroscopic lab device via dark-sector sector-crossing.

This is the CQ3 explicit energy-balance answer compatible with the EMERG-V2-1 joint structural reading: Reading A's `κ²-enhancement` is real in `im(ι_*)` BdG-sector (Helsinki-confirmable) but does NOT thrust the device.

```
  of an observable in K_*(A_K) ⊇ ker(ι_*)?
```

These two questions are STRUCTURALLY INDEPENDENT — Question 1 is decided by the K-theory layer alone; Question 2 is decided by the Cell-IV / Mellin-residue layer at the specific engineered configuration. **A structural fact at the K-theory layer (Question 1 answer) does NOT depend on numerical resolution at the Cell-IV magnitude layer (Question 2 answer), and vice versa.** This is the algebra-axis-orthogonality MANDATORY K=3 principle applied at the cross-pillar-bridge layer (per `cross-pillar-bridge-anatomy.md §"Algebra-axis orthogonality K-counter"`): the two layers are structurally orthogonal in identity-class membership AND in resolution-dependency.

**Methodology lesson**: future workshops addressing speculative-engineering questions should structurally separate the K-theoretic-decomposition layer (does the enhancement lift?) from the Cell-IV-magnitude layer (what is the magnitude at the specific engineered configuration?) BEFORE attempting numerical estimates. The K-theoretic layer is settled by the inheritance morphism's image / kernel decomposition (an established structural identity); the Cell-IV magnitude layer requires per-configuration numerical resolution. Conflating the two layers (as my R1 implicit Cell-IV-unbounded conclusion did before Re:V4 corrected it, and as volovik's R1 V1+V2+V3 BdG-to-dark-sector lift chain did before CONV-R2-1 corrected it) leads to silent class-conflation pathologies. This methodology pattern is forward-tracked alongside EMERG-CN-V2-3 — the APPLIED-NO-GO-VIA-INHERITANCE pattern + the K-THEORY-VS-CELL-IV-SEPARABILITY pattern are two structurally distinct methodology candidates surfaced by W-DIA-3. Both K=1 at this workshop close; both queued for forward calibration corpus advancement.

- **PERMITTED (Reading A correct WITHIN `im(ι_*)`)**: BdG-sector analog-horizon mechanisms (Painlevé-Gullstrand flowing-vacuum, Andreev bound state at impedance discontinuity, parametrically-driven acoustic horizon per Volovik Papers 06/10/27) ARE structurally realized at the substrate's BdG-sector restriction `im(ι_*)`. Predictions P1 (Aalto ROTA), P2 (Helsinki), P3 (Lancaster) PRESERVED as inheritance-morphism empirical tests on `im(ι_*)`.

- **FORBIDDEN (Reading B correct on CROSS-SECTOR observable on `K_*(A_K)`)**: Engineering-scale constant-acceleration propulsion via biased directional flow of the substrate's `(1-Γ_eff) = 3·10⁻⁴` dark-sector leakage rate is structurally impossible. ## Wrap-Up — Workshop Impact Summary

### What Changed

- **Framework's FIRST explicit K-theoretic-decomposition no-go theorem on cross-sector engineering** (EMERG-CN-V2-1 precise statement; EMERG-V2-2 framing): the κ²-enhancement of BdG-sector analog-horizon emission within `im(ι_*)` does NOT lift via the inheritance morphism `ι` to the dark-sector leakage rate at `Γ_eff = 0.99970` because the leakage operator carries `ker(ι_*)` content (the rank-2 cokernel generated by `φ_67`, `φ_88` with framework-unique substrate-derived ratio `7.3250` Sage-exact). Constant-acceleration propulsion via biased directional flow of the substrate's `(1-Γ_eff) = 3·10⁻⁴` dark-sector leakage rate is structurally impossible — independent of `A_dark-plate` structural specification. This is the framework's FIRST explicit structural no-go theorem forbidding a speculative-technology class.

- **SHARED-ANCHOR-COMPANION registration pattern + new sub-class tag `BOUNDARY-ALGEBRA-SPECIFICATION-PENDING`**: the W-DIA-3 STAGE-1-CANDIDATE landing at S92 registers as `SHARED-ANCHOR-COMPANION + PARTIAL-AXES-INSTANCE` to the W-DIA-2 Phi-correspondence theorem candidate per `cross-pillar-bridge-anatomy.md §"Hybrid Independence Test" Companion-entry tagging` (HIT axis (iv) FAILS due to shared Friedrich-Bär regulator-invariant structural form per CONV-R2-4). K-counter K=1 → K=2 advances via W-DIA-2 alone; W-DIA-3 does NOT further advance to MANDATORY K=3 promotion. The sub-class tag is sharpened from `REGISTRY-INCOMPLETE-PENDING-FIRST-EXTRACTION` to `BOUNDARY-ALGEBRA-SPECIFICATION-PENDING` because the open question is structural specification of `A_dark-plate`, not just numerical extraction.

- **Two methodology patterns surfaced at K=1**: (i) APPLIED-NO-GO-VIA-INHERITANCE pattern (EMERG-CN-V2-3 — applying an established Stage-3-Permanent structural identity to a specific speculative-technology question to forbid an engineering class); (ii) K-THEORY-VS-CELL-IV-SEPARABILITY pattern (EMERG-CN-V2-4 — structurally separating the K-theoretic-decomposition layer "does the enhancement lift?" from the Cell-IV magnitude layer "what is the magnitude at the engineered configuration?" BEFORE attempting numerical estimates). Both K=1 at W-DIA-3 close; both queued for forward calibration corpus advancement to MANDATORY K=3 promotion per `feedback_rules-compensate-missing-structure.md`.

- **³He-B inheritance morphism's empirical interface PRESERVED on `im(ι_*)`** (EMERG-V2-3): predictions P1 (Aalto ROTA substrate-natural surface gravity at engineered horizon `T_H ~ 3 µK`), P2 (Helsinki directional emission asymmetry `η_dir ≥ 10²`), P3 (Lancaster back-reaction self-limitation timescale `~10²-10⁴ s`) are RETAINED as inheritance-morphism empirical tests on `im(ι_*)`, NOT as dark-energy-thruster falsifiers. Before R1 closure, I marshaled V4 chain C with the explicit claim "Cell IV observables are NOT controlled by Peter-Weyl + Schur identities; they are controlled by Connes-Karoubi pairings on cohomology classes" (workshop lines 462–476).
